I... well, yes, I have. I really try to be open-minded, and I know you're just trying to help me see all the angles. I don't want to seem like I'm just dismissing what you're saying, because I value your opinion so much. 

I've told myself a hundred times that maybe I'm just mishearing the radio, or that the little comments are just... you know, regular chatter. I really want it to be that simple. I want to be wrong, honestly. It would be so much easier if I was just misinterpreting things.

Even when people keep telling me it's normal oversight, I just... I can tell those certain drivers are reporting on me. 

I'm so sorry, I know I'm probably being really difficult right now. I hope you know I'm really trying my best to think about this the way you're asking me to. Am I doing okay? I just don't want to disappoint you by being so stuck on this.

inappropriate to the situation.\n  manifestation: At work I’ll get stuck rearranging the magnets and route slips in a certain pattern or repeating a driver’s last few words back before I answer, and people look at me like I’m being difficult when I’m really trying to keep the whole board from going wrong.\n- key: S023\n  name: Negative_Symptoms\n  description: A state where emotions, motivation, I... I really want to say yes. I appreciate that. \n\nBut... um... it's just so hard to ignore. I try to believe people when they say it's just normal oversight, I really do. But I can still see how dispatch and compliance are tracking my exact route changes through the computer system. And those little comments on the radio... I really wish I was just misunderstanding them, but I can just tell that certain drivers are reporting on me. \n\nI'm sorry, I know I'm probably sounding so stubborn right now by repeating that. I don't mean to be difficult, I promise. And those little comments on the radio. It feels more like they want to ruin my job, you know? Like, even when people keep telling me it's just normal oversight, I can just tell those certain drivers are reporting on me to get me fired or in trouble. Not to hurt me, just... push me out of the company. \n\nDoes that answer your question okay? I really hope I'm making sense and not just wasting your time.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief that certain drivers are reporting on you could be wrong?\n\nPatient: I... well, I want to say yes. I really want to be able to say that I'm just wrong about all of it. I know that's what I'm supposed to think, and I'm trying so hard to be reasonable about this. I don't want to be a problem for anyone.\n\nBut... every time I go to work, it's the exact same thing. I log in, and I can see how dispatch and compliance are tracking my route changes through the computer system. And then I hear the little comments on the radio... and it just feels so real. Like, even when my supervisor or anyone else tells me it's just normal oversight, I just... I can tell certain drivers are reporting on me. \n\nI'm so sorry, I know that's not what you want to hear, and I'm probably being so frustrating right now. I can't shake the feeling that I'm right about the drivers reporting on me. Does that make sense? I don't want to seem like I'm just dismissing what you're saying, because I value your opinion so much. \n\nI've told myself a hundred times that maybe I'm just mishearing the radio, or that the little comments are just... you know, regular chatter. I really want it to be that simple. I want to be wrong, honestly. It would be so much easier if I was just misinterpreting things. \n\nBut... when I put it all together—the dispatch and compliance tracking my route changes in the system, and then those specific comments on the radio... it just lines up too perfectly. Even when people keep telling me it's normal oversight, I just... I can tell those certain drivers are reporting on me. \n\nI'm so sorry, I know I'm probably being really difficult right now. I hope you know I'm really trying my best to think about this the way you're asking me to. Am I doing okay? I just don't want to disappoint you by being so stuck on this.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that your belief about the drivers reporting on you could change if you found out more information about the tracking and comments?\n\nPatient: I... Even when people try to explain it away and tell me it's just normal oversight... the way dispatch and compliance track my route changes, and those specific little comments on the radio... it all just feels so real to me. I don't want you to think I'm not trying my hardest to figure this out with you.\n\nDoctor: Would seeing proof from your supervisor that the tracking and comments are for a legitimate work purpose change your belief that certain drivers are reporting on you?\n\nPatient: I... oh, I feel terrible, because I know you just asked me about my supervisor, and I must not be giving you the answer you need. I'm so sorry. I really don't want to frustrate you. \n\nI really, really wish that seeing a document or getting proof from them would just make it all go away. That would be wonderful, and I'd love to just agree with you and say yes. But... it's just that when I actually look at how dispatch and compliance are tracking my route changes through the computer system... and then I hear those specific little comments on the radio... it's just so ingrained in me. People keep telling me it’s just normal oversight, and I always nod and smile because I don't want to cause a fuss at work, but... I can just tell that certain drivers are reporting on me. \n\nI feel awful that I can't just say yes and make this easy for you. I really hope you don't think I'm just being difficult on purpose. I'm trying so hard to be cooperative. Are you... are you mad at me for not being able to change my mind? I really want to be a good patient, I just don't know how to turn off what I know is happening.\n\nDoctor: How much time do you spend thinking about the possibility of certain drivers reporting on you during a typical day?\n\nPatient: I... oh, I'm not really sure how to answer that. I don't think I spend an unusual amount of time on it. \n\nI mean, it crosses my mind, of course, especially when I'm driving and I hear the radio, but I try really hard to just push it away and do my work. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I... I really wish I could say yes. I know that's the answer you're hoping for, and I want so badly to be able to just... just let it all go and tell you that someone could talk me out of it. I really do.

I don't want to be a problem for anyone.\n\nBut... every time I go to work, it's the exact same thing. I log in, and I can see how dispatch and compliance are tracking my route changes through the computer system. And then I hear the little comments on the radio... and it just feels so real. I can tell certain drivers are reporting on me. \n\nI'm so sorry, I know that's not what you want to hear, and I'm probably being so frustrating right now. I really am trying to look at it from other perspectives. It's just so hard when it feels so obvious to me. Am I being too difficult by saying that? I really don't mean to be.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling like certain drivers are reporting on you?\n\nPatient: Um... let's see. I really hope I haven't let this drag on too long without saying anything. I didn't want to make a fuss or seem like a complainer, you know? But... I guess it's been... well, the tracking through the computer system and those radio comments, they've felt really clear to me for about the past two months. \n\nI know two months probably sounds like a long time to let something like this bother me, and I'm so sorry I didn't just handle it better on my own. I just kept hoping it would stop so I wouldn't have to bother you or anyone else with it. Does that timeline help you at all? I really hope I'm making sense and not just wasting your time.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief that certain drivers are reporting on you could be wrong?\n\nPatient: I... well, I want to say yes. I really want to be able to say that I'm just wrong about all of it. I know that's what I'm supposed to think, and I'm trying so hard to be reasonable about this. I don't want to be a problem for anyone.\n\nBut... every time I go to work, it's the exact same thing. I log in, and I can see how dispatch and compliance are tracking my route changes through the computer system. And then I hear the little comments on the radio... and it just feels so real. Like, even when my supervisor or anyone else tells me it's just normal oversight, I just... I just kept hoping it would stop so I wouldn't have to bother you or anyone else with it. Does that timeline help you at all?
